   268	        this(listenerName, maxReceiveSize, memoryPool, connectionsMaxIdleNanos, time, 0, java.util.Collections.emptyMap());
   269	    }
   270	
   271	    /** Test-only constructor: empty configs map (default principal builder). */
   272	    public IoUringSelector(ListenerName listenerName,
   273	                           int maxReceiveSize,
   274	                           MemoryPool memoryPool,
   275	                           long connectionsMaxIdleNanos,
   276	                           Time time,
   277	                           int processorId) {
   278	        this(listenerName, maxReceiveSize, memoryPool, connectionsMaxIdleNanos, time, processorId, java.util.Collections.emptyMap());
   279	    }
   280	
   281	    public IoUringSelector(ListenerName listenerName,
   282	                           int maxReceiveSize,
   283	                           MemoryPool memoryPool,
   284	                           long connectionsMaxIdleNanos,
   285	                           Time time,
   286	                           int processorId,
   287	                           Map<String, ?> configs) {
   288	        this.listenerName = Objects.requireNonNull(listenerName, "listenerName");
   289	        this.maxReceiveSize = maxReceiveSize;
   290	        this.memoryPool = Objects.requireNonNull(memoryPool, "memoryPool");
   291	        this.connectionsMaxIdleNanos = connectionsMaxIdleNanos;
   292	        this.time = Objects.requireNonNull(time, "time");
   293	        this.processorId = processorId;
   294	        this.configs = Objects.requireNonNull(configs, "configs");
   295	        this.maxPendingAccepts = DEFAULT_MAX_PENDING_ACCEPTS;
   296	    }
   297	
   298	    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   299	    // Event-loop hooks (package-private; called from the Netty event-loop thread)
   300	    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   301	
   302	    /**
   303	     * Register a freshly accepted Netty channel. Builds the full {@link KafkaChannel}
   304	     * stack and queues it for the next {@link #poll(long)}.
   305	     */
   306	    void onAccept(Channel nettyChannel, InetSocketAddress remote, InetSocketAddress local) {
   307	        if (closed) {
   308	            nettyChannel.close();
   309	            return;
   310	        }
   311	        // Bound the accept queue depth: if the Processor is slow to drain pendingAccepts,
   312	        // close the freshly accepted netty channel immediately rather than building the
   313	        // full KafkaChannel stack (TransportLayer, Authenticator, MetadataRegistry, plus
   314	        // a slot in nettyChannels) and stranding it in an unbounded queue. This mirrors
   315	        // NIO's per-Processor bounded queue (ArrayBlockingQueue(connectionQueueSize) in
   316	        // SocketServer.Acceptor) — NIO blocks on put() when full so the OS backlog
   317	        // absorbs the pressure; we close because blocking the Netty event loop would
   318	        // freeze every child channel served by this listener.
   319	        //
   320	        // Closing here sends a TCP RST (or FIN) to the client. Without the cap, a stuck
   321	        // Processor would let the queue grow until the JVM OOMs — every entry pins a
   322	        // Netty Channel with its own direct-memory recvByteBufAllocator state.
   323	        if (pendingAcceptCount.get() >= maxPendingAccepts) {
   324	            log.info("io_uring: dropping accept from {} — pending-accept queue at cap ({}); " +
   325	                     "Processor likely overloaded", remote, maxPendingAccepts);
   326	            nettyChannel.close();
   327	            return;
   328	        }
   329	        // Format must match ServerConnectionId so Processor.processDisconnected can parse it
   330	        // and decrement ConnectionQuotas correctly. Using the synthetic "iouring-N" form
   331	        // would silently break quota release.
   332	        int connectionIndex = idGen.getAndUpdate(i -> i == Integer.MAX_VALUE ? 0 : i + 1);
   333	        String id = local.getAddress().getHostAddress() + ":" + local.getPort() + "-"
   334	                  + remote.getAddress().getHostAddress() + ":" + remote.getPort() + "-"
   335	                  + processorId + "-" + connectionIndex;
   336	        // Pass wakeup::release as the write-completion callback. Netty fires writeAndFlush's
   337	        // listener on the event-loop thread, NOT the Processor thread — so the listener must
   338	        // re-arm the Processor's poll() semaphore, otherwise a small Send (no watermark
   339	        // crossing → no onWritabilityChanged) leaves poll() asleep until timeoutMs while
   340	        // completedSends/RESPONSE_SENT are silently pending.
   341	        IoUringTransportLayer transport = new IoUringTransportLayer(nettyChannel, remote, local, wakeup::release);
   342	        Authenticator authenticator = new IoUringPlaintextAuthenticator(transport, listenerName, configs);
   343	        IoUringChannelMetadataRegistry metadata = new IoUringChannelMetadataRegistry();
   344	        // Mirror NIO Selector.register: seed ClientInformation.EMPTY so the first RequestContext
   345	        // (built before any ApiVersionsRequest is parsed) never captures null.
   346	        metadata.registerClientInformation(ClientInformation.EMPTY);
   347	        KafkaChannel channel = new KafkaChannel(id, transport, () -> authenticator, maxReceiveSize, memoryPool, metadata);
   348	
   349	        nettyChannel.attr(TRANSPORT_ATTR).set(transport);
   350	        nettyChannel.attr(CHANNEL_ID_ATTR).set(id);
   351	
   352	        // Stash the netty channel so mute/unmute can flip autoRead without chasing
   353	        // references through the KafkaChannel's selectionKey.
   354	        nettyChannels.put(id, nettyChannel);
   355	        pendingAccepts.offer(channel);
   356	        pendingAcceptCount.incrementAndGet();
   357	        wakeup.release();
   358	    }
   359	
   360	    /** Forward bytes from the event loop into the transport's inbound queue. */
   361	    void onRead(Channel nettyChannel, ByteBuf buf) {
   362	        IoUringTransportLayer transport = nettyChannel.attr(TRANSPORT_ATTR).get();
   363	        if (transport == null) {
   364	            buf.release();
   365	            return;
   366	        }
   367	        transport.offerInbound(buf);
   368	        wakeup.release();
   369	    }
   370	
   371	    /** Mark EOF on the transport and enqueue the channel id for graceful removal. */
   372	    void onDisconnect(Channel nettyChannel) {
   373	        IoUringTransportLayer transport = nettyChannel.attr(TRANSPORT_ATTR).get();
   374	        if (transport != null) transport.markEof();
   375	        String id = nettyChannel.attr(CHANNEL_ID_ATTR).get();
   376	        if (id != null) pendingDisconnects.offer(id);
   377	        wakeup.release();
   378	    }
   379	
   380	    /**
   381	     * Netty's outbound buffer flipped from unwritable to writable (or vice-versa). Wake
   382	     * any blocking poll so {@link KafkaChannel#write()} retries.
   383	     */
   384	    void onWritabilityChanged(Channel nettyChannel) {
   385	        wakeup.release();
   386	    }
   387	
   388	    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   389	    // Selectable / BrokerSelector API (called from the Processor thread)
   390	    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   391	
   392	    @Override
   393	    public void poll(long timeoutMs) throws IOException {
   394	        if (closed) throw new IOException("selector is closed");
   395	
   396	        // Reset per-poll outputs BEFORE evicting closingChannels: the eviction populates
   397	        // disconnected with LOCAL_CLOSE for each evicted id, mirroring NIO's
   398	        // {@code Selector.clear} which calls {@code addToDisconnected} during eviction.
   399	        completedReceives.clear();
   400	        completedSends.clear();
   401	        disconnected.clear();
   402	        connected.clear();
   403	        justAccepted.clear();
   404	        receivesThisPoll.clear();
   405	
   406	        // MemoryPool recovery: re-admit channels that self-muted due to memory pressure
   407	        // once the pool reports available again. Helper extracted to keep poll() under
   408	        // checkstyle's MethodLength limit.
   409	        recoverFromMemoryPressure();
   410	
   411	        // Drain closingChannels left over from the previous poll. Helper extracted for
   412	        // both readability and to keep poll() under checkstyle's MethodLength limit.
   413	        drainClosingChannels();
   414	
   415	        // Any failedSends not absorbed by the closingChannels eviction above are NEW
   416	        // failures (e.g. setSend threw on a healthy channel) — surface them as the
   417	        // disconnect notification the Processor expects.
   418	        for (String id : failedSends) {
   419	            disconnected.put(id, ChannelState.FAILED_SEND);
   420	        }
   421	        failedSends.clear();
   422	
   423	        // Capture once, BEFORE I/O, so a channel that progresses this poll never expires this poll.
   424	        long nowNanos = time.nanoseconds();
   425	
   426	        boolean madeProgress = false;
   427	
   428	        // 1. Drain accepts.
   429	        KafkaChannel acceptedChannel;
   430	        while ((acceptedChannel = pendingAccepts.poll()) != null) {
   431	            pendingAcceptCount.decrementAndGet();
   432	            channels.put(acceptedChannel.id(), acceptedChannel);
   433	            lastActiveNanos.put(acceptedChannel.id(), nowNanos);
   434	            // PLAINTEXT auth completes synchronously; one prepare() call drives the channel to READY.
   435	            try {
   436	                acceptedChannel.prepare();
   437	            } catch (Exception e) {
   438	                log.debug("PLAINTEXT prepare unexpectedly failed for {}", acceptedChannel.id(), e);
   439	            }
   440	            connected.add(acceptedChannel.id());
   441	            justAccepted.add(acceptedChannel.id());
   442	            madeProgress = true;
   443	        }
   444	
   445	        // 2. For each active channel, do at most one read and one write step.
   446	        //    Channels in justAccepted (added this same poll's step 1) are deferred until
   447	        //    the next poll — SocketServer must run applyConnectionQuotasForNewlyAcceptedChannels
   448	        //    against the freshly-connected channel before we surface any receive from it.
   449	        for (Iterator<Map.Entry<String, KafkaChannel>> it = channels.entrySet().iterator(); it.hasNext(); ) {
   450	            Map.Entry<String, KafkaChannel> entry = it.next();
   451	            KafkaChannel channel = entry.getValue();
   452	            if (!channel.ready()) continue;
   453	            if (justAccepted.contains(channel.id())) continue;
   454	
   455	            // Read step. KafkaChannel.isMuted() is the source of truth: mute(id) above calls
   456	            // through to KafkaChannel.mute() via KafkaChannelMuteBridge, which sets muteState
   457	            // and removes OP_READ; the transport layer translates the OP_READ removal into
   458	            // Netty autoRead=false. So a muted channel here has both kernel-level backpressure
   459	            // (no more inbound from Netty) and a state-machine gate (so we never produce a
   460	            // completedReceive while the Processor expects MUTED or MUTED_AND_RESPONSE_PENDING).
   461	            if (!channel.isMuted()) {
   462	                try {
   463	                    long read = channel.read();
   464	                    if (read > 0) {
   465	                        lastActiveNanos.put(channel.id(), nowNanos);
   466	                        madeProgress = true;
   467	                    }
   468	                    NetworkReceive completed = channel.maybeCompleteReceive();
   469	                    if (completed != null) {
   470	                        completedReceives.add(completed);
   471	                        receivesThisPoll.add(channel.id());
   472	                        madeProgress = true;
   473	                    }
   474	                    // Self-mute detection: KafkaChannel.read() flips muteState to MUTED when
   475	                    // memoryPool.tryAllocate returns null. If now muted and NOT operator-muted,
   476	                    // the read self-muted us. Flag outOfMemory so the next poll's
   477	                    // recoverFromMemoryPressure() walks channels. Mirrors NIO Selector:691-692.
   478	                    if (channel.isMuted() && !explicitlyMutedChannels.contains(channel)) {
   479	                        outOfMemory = true;
   480	                    }
   481	                } catch (Exception e) {
   482	                    // Catch Exception, not just IOException: KafkaChannel.read() declares
   483	                    // throws IOException, but NetworkReceive throws InvalidReceiveException
   484	                    // (a KafkaException -> RuntimeException) when the wire reports a negative
   485	                    // size or a size > socket.request.max.bytes. Catching only IOException
   486	                    // lets that escape poll() so Processor.poll() never sees the failure,
   487	                    // the channel is never closed, the connection quota is never decremented,
   488	                    // and the unread Netty ByteBufs accumulate until idle expiry or direct-
   489	                    // memory failure. Matches NIO Selector.pollSelectionKeys's catch (Exception).
   490	                    log.debug("Read failed on channel {}", channel.id(), e);
   491	                    enqueueClose(channel.id(), ChannelState.LOCAL_CLOSE);
   492	                    it.remove();
   493	                    lastActiveNanos.remove(channel.id());
   494	                    continue;
   495	                }
   496	            }
   497	
   498	            // Write step.
   499	            if (channel.hasSend()) {
   500	                try {
   501	                    long written = channel.write();
   502	                    if (written > 0) {
   503	                        lastActiveNanos.put(channel.id(), nowNanos);
   504	                        madeProgress = true;
   505	                    }
   506	                    NetworkSend send = channel.maybeCompleteSend();
   507	                    if (send != null) {
   508	                        completedSends.add(send);
   509	                        madeProgress = true;
   510	                    }
   511	                } catch (Exception e) {
   512	                    // Same rationale as the read path: catch Exception, not just IOException,
   513	                    // so a RuntimeException from inside the send chain (e.g. an outbound
   514	                    // ByteBufferSend tripping over a malformed message) routes through
   515	                    // FAILED_SEND instead of escaping poll() and stranding the channel.
   516	                    log.debug("Write failed on channel {}", channel.id(), e);
   517	                    enqueueClose(channel.id(), ChannelState.FAILED_SEND);
   518	                    it.remove();
   519	                    lastActiveNanos.remove(channel.id());
   520	                }
   521	            }
   522	        }
   523	
   524	        // 3. Drain disconnects — but give buffered bytes one last delivery pass first,
   525	        //    mirroring NIO's closingChannels semantics. The channel goes into
   526	        //    closingChannels so this poll's completedReceives still resolve via
   527	        //    closingChannel(id) on the Processor side; we close the channel for real at the
   528	        //    start of the next poll.
   529	        if (drainPendingDisconnects()) {
   530	            madeProgress = true;
   531	        }
   532	
   533	        // 4. Idle expiry — only against channels that haven't already been claimed by disconnect.
   534	        if (connectionsMaxIdleNanos > 0) {
   535	            Iterator<Map.Entry<String, Long>> idleIt = lastActiveNanos.entrySet().iterator();
   536	            while (idleIt.hasNext()) {
   537	                Map.Entry<String, Long> entry = idleIt.next();
   538	                if (nowNanos - entry.getValue() > connectionsMaxIdleNanos) {
   539	                    KafkaChannel channel = channels.remove(entry.getKey());
   540	                    nettyChannels.remove(entry.getKey());
   541	                    if (channel != null) {
   542	                        explicitlyMutedChannels.remove(channel);
   543	                        disconnected.put(entry.getKey(), ChannelState.EXPIRED);
   544	                        Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "expired channel");
   545	                    }
   546	                    idleIt.remove();
   547	                    madeProgress = true;
   548	                }
   549	            }
   550	        }
   551	
   552	        // 5. If nothing happened, wait. Drain permits on acquisition so a burst of
   553	        //    event-loop notifications doesn't produce N consecutive zero-work polls.
   554	        if (!madeProgress && timeoutMs > 0) {
   555	            acquireWithTimeout(timeoutMs);
   556	        } else {
   557	            // We did work — but a wakeup may have queued more events. Don't sleep, but
   558	            // also don't leave stale permits around for the next poll's wait condition.
   559	            wakeup.drainPermits();
   560	        }

   907	    @Override
   908	    public KafkaChannel lowestPriorityChannel() {
   909	        // Mirrors org.apache.kafka.common.network.Selector.lowestPriorityChannel():
   910	        //   1. A channel already in teardown — evicting one of those is "free" and never
   911	        //      sacrifices a healthy connection.
   912	        //   2. The least-recently-active channel — its peer hasn't done anything for the
   913	        //      longest, so closing it loses the least useful state. Important so that
   914	        //      the controller and replication peers (constantly active) survive eviction
   915	        //      under broker-wide max.connections pressure on a PLAINTEXT listener.
   916	        //   3. Any channel — degenerate fallback if neither table has an entry.
   917	        // Previously this returned `channels.values().iterator().next()` which, given
   918	        // the LinkedHashMap insertion order, would target the OLDEST connection (most
   919	        // likely the inter-broker controller/replication peer that connected at boot).
   920	        if (!closingChannels.isEmpty()) {
   921	            return closingChannels.values().iterator().next();
   922	        }
   923	        if (!lastActiveNanos.isEmpty()) {
   924	            // Find the channel with the smallest lastActiveNanos. The map is updated on
   925	            // every read/write step in poll() so it tracks per-channel liveness accurately.
   926	            String oldestId = null;
   927	            long oldestNanos = Long.MAX_VALUE;
   928	            for (Map.Entry<String, Long> entry : lastActiveNanos.entrySet()) {
   929	                long nanos = entry.getValue();
   930	                if (nanos < oldestNanos) {
   931	                    oldestNanos = nanos;
   932	                    oldestId = entry.getKey();
   933	                }
   934	            }
   935	            if (oldestId != null) {
   936	                KafkaChannel channel = channels.get(oldestId);
   937	                if (channel != null) return channel;
   938	            }
   939	        }
   940	        return channels.isEmpty() ? null : channels.values().iterator().next();
   941	    }

   958	    @Override
   959	    public void close() {
   960	        if (closed) return;
   961	        closed = true;
   962	        for (KafkaChannel channel : channels.values()) {
   963	            Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "channel on selector close");
   964	        }
   965	        channels.clear();
   966	        for (KafkaChannel channel : closingChannels.values()) {
   967	            Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "closing channel on selector close");
   968	        }
   969	        closingChannels.clear();
   970	        explicitlyMutedChannels.clear();
   971	        nettyChannels.clear();
   972	        lastActiveNanos.clear();
   973	        // Drain queues so any in-flight ByteBufs are released.
   974	        KafkaChannel pending;
   975	        while ((pending = pendingAccepts.poll()) != null) {
   976	            pendingAcceptCount.decrementAndGet();
   977	            Utils.closeQuietly(pending, "pending-accept on close");
   978	        }
   979	        pendingDisconnects.clear();
   980	        wakeup.release();
   981	    }
   982	
   983	    @Override
   984	    public void close(String id) {
   985	        KafkaChannel channel = channels.remove(id);
   986	        nettyChannels.remove(id);
   987	        lastActiveNanos.remove(id);
   988	        if (channel != null) {
   989	            explicitlyMutedChannels.remove(channel);
   990	            Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "channel close(" + id + ")");
   991	            return;
   992	        }
   993	        // Mirror NIO's Selector.close(id) (clients/.../Selector.java:886-899): if the
   994	        // channel is not in the active map, look in closingChannels and clean it up
   995	        // there. Without this, closeExcessConnections (called from the Acceptor under
   996	        // broker-max pressure) can repeatedly select the same closing channel via
   997	        // lowestPriorityChannel() — closingChannels stays populated until the NEXT
   998	        // poll's eviction, and within the same poll there is no other way to drain it.
   999	        // Equally important: the quota for closing channels was already `dec`'d when
  1000	        // they surfaced in `disconnected`. If close(id) re-routed through any path that
  1001	        // re-applies `dec`, the counter would underflow on every excess-eviction race.
  1002	        // Keeping the cleanup local to the selector and silent to the outside (no
  1003	        // entry in `disconnected`) preserves that one-dec-per-channel invariant.
  1004	        KafkaChannel closing = closingChannels.remove(id);
  1005	        if (closing != null) {
  1006	            explicitlyMutedChannels.remove(closing);
  1007	            failedSends.remove(id);
  1008	            Utils.closeQuietly(closing, "closing channel close(" + id + ")");
  1009	        }
  1010	    }

   561	    }
   562	
   563	    /**
   564	     * If the previous poll observed a channel self-mute because {@code memoryPool.tryAllocate}
   565	     * returned null (KafkaChannel.read() flips muteState to MUTED when the pool is dry), and
   566	     * the pool now reports available again, walk every channel and call {@code maybeUnmute()}
   567	     * — but ONLY for channels NOT in {@link #explicitlyMutedChannels} (those stay muted
   568	     * because the request pipeline is still throttling them, not because of memory pressure).
   569	     *
   570	     * <p>Mirrors NIO {@code Selector.poll()} at clients/.../Selector.java:457-466. Without
   571	     * this recovery loop, channels that self-mute under {@code queued.max.bytes} pressure
   572	     * stay MUTED until idle expiry (~10 min default) and the broker effectively wedges its
   573	     * PLAINTEXT listener under sustained memory pressure.
   574	     */
   575	    private void recoverFromMemoryPressure() {
   576	        if (!memoryPool.isOutOfMemory() && outOfMemory) {
   577	            log.trace("io_uring selector recovering from memory pressure — unmuting self-muted channels");
   578	            for (KafkaChannel channel : channels.values()) {
   579	                if (channel.isInMutableState() && !explicitlyMutedChannels.contains(channel)) {
   580	                    KafkaChannelMuteBridge.maybeUnmute(channel);
   581	                }
   582	            }
   583	            outOfMemory = false;
   584	        }
   585	    }
   586	
   587	    /**
   588	     * Drain {@link #closingChannels} left over from the previous poll. NIO's
   589	     * {@code Selector.clear} at {@code clients/.../Selector.java#842-863} is the model:
   590	     * a channel stays in {@code closingChannels} as long as
   591	     * <ul>
   592	     *   <li>{@link #failedSends} did not fire for it this poll (the FAILED_SEND
   593	     *       notification is the terminal signal — no further reads should be attempted),
   594	     *       AND</li>
   595	     *   <li>there is more buffered work we can still deliver: either the channel is
   596	     *       muted (the Processor will read it after explicit unmute) OR one more read
   597	     *       can produce a completedReceive.</li>
   598	     * </ul>
   599	     * <p>Evict only when nothing more is forthcoming, and at *that* point emit the
   600	     * disconnect notification. This is what defers {@code disconnected} from the FIN
   601	     * poll (where the channel still has buffered work) to the eviction poll, matching
   602	     * NIO and closing the same-poll double-dec window
   603	     * ({@code processDisconnected} + {@code closeExcessConnections}-&gt;{@code close}).
   604	     * For pipelined requests followed by FIN this drain is what surfaces R2, R3, …
   605	     * across successive polls — without it, only R1 reaches the request queue.
   606	     */
   607	    private void drainClosingChannels() {
   608	        if (closingChannels.isEmpty()) return;
   609	        Iterator<Map.Entry<String, KafkaChannel>> it = closingChannels.entrySet().iterator();
   610	        while (it.hasNext()) {
   611	            Map.Entry<String, KafkaChannel> entry = it.next();
   612	            String id = entry.getKey();
   613	            KafkaChannel channel = entry.getValue();
   614	            boolean sendFailed = failedSends.remove(id);
   615	            boolean keepClosing = false;
   616	            if (!sendFailed && channel.ready()) {
   617	                if (explicitlyMutedChannels.contains(channel)) {
   618	                    // Operator-muted closing channel: leave for next poll. The Processor must
   619	                    // unmute it explicitly before the final buffered receives surface, exactly
   620	                    // as NIO does at {@code Selector.java#702}. We intentionally do NOT short-
   621	                    // circuit on the broader {@code channel.isMuted()}: a channel can also be
   622	                    // self-muted (memory-pool pressure inside {@link KafkaChannel#read}). Self-
   623	                    // muted closing channels must still attempt to drain, otherwise a peer FIN
   624	                    // arriving while {@code queued.max.bytes} is saturated would strand the
   625	                    // channel here forever — {@link #recoverFromMemoryPressure} only walks
   626	                    // {@link #channels}, not {@link #closingChannels}, so the channel never
   627	                    // gets unmuted, never has its connection quota decremented, and Netty's
   628	                    // inbound {@code ByteBuf}s queued on the transport layer leak indefinitely.
   629	                    keepClosing = true;
   630	                } else {
   631	                    try {
   632	                        channel.read();
   633	                        NetworkReceive completed = channel.maybeCompleteReceive();
   634	                        if (completed != null) {
   635	                            completedReceives.add(completed);
   636	                            receivesThisPoll.add(id);
   637	                            keepClosing = true;
   638	                        }
   639	                    } catch (Exception e) {
   640	                        // Catch Exception, not just IOException: an InvalidReceiveException
   641	                        // (RuntimeException) here means the final-read tripped on a malformed
   642	                        // size prefix. We just want to evict the channel and let the next poll's
   643	                        // closingChannel(id) path be a no-op — escaping would leak the channel.
   644	                        log.trace("Read from closing channel {} failed, evicting", id, e);
   645	                    }
   646	                }
   647	            }
   648	            if (!keepClosing) {
   649	                explicitlyMutedChannels.remove(channel);
   650	                disconnected.put(id, ChannelState.LOCAL_CLOSE);
   651	                Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "closing channel evicted");
   652	                it.remove();
   653	            }
   654	        }
   655	    }
   656	
   657	    /**
   658	     * Step 3 of {@link #poll(long)}: drain {@link #pendingDisconnects}, surface each id
   659	     * in {@link #disconnected}, and stash the channel in {@link #closingChannels} so the
   660	     * Processor can still resolve any final completedReceives via
   661	     * {@code closingChannel(id)} before the next poll evicts it.
   662	     *
   663	     * <p>Two invariants are enforced here:
   664	     * <ul>
   665	     *   <li><b>same-poll accept+disconnect:</b> a channel in {@link #justAccepted} that
   666	     *       FINs before the Processor has run {@code applyConnectionQuotasForNewlyAcceptedChannels}
   667	     *       is dropped silently (no {@code disconnected} entry, removed from
   668	     *       {@code connected}). Surfacing it would let {@code processDisconnected} call
   669	     *       {@code connectionQuotas.dec} on a counter that was never {@code inc}'d.
   670	     *   <li><b>one completedReceive per channel per poll:</b> the final-read drain is
   671	     *       skipped if step 2 already produced a receive for this id. Remaining buffered
   672	     *       bytes stay readable through {@code closingChannel(id)} for one more poll —
   673	     *       same as NIO's {@code Selector.clear()}.
   674	     *   <li><b>muted channels do not produce a final receive:</b> a channel that was
   675	     *       muted (operator or MemoryPool) at FIN time must not have its inbound queue
   676	     *       drained here. The Processor explicitly relies on the invariant that the
   677	     *       request flow stays paused until {@code unmute()} is called, and muting
   678	     *       only blocks new kernel pushes, not bytes already in the transport queue. NIO
   679	     *       enforces this via {@code maybeReadFromClosingChannel} skipping muted channels
   680	     *       at {@code Selector.java#698}. We defer the buffered bytes to the next poll's
   681	     *       {@code closingChannel(id)} resolution path, where the channel will be
   682	     *       drained only after the operator unmutes — matching NIO precisely.
   683	     * </ul>
   684	     *
   685	     * @return {@code true} if any channel transitioned to disconnected (or any silent
   686	     *         drop happened), so the caller can mark progress and skip the wait at the
   687	     *         end of {@link #poll(long)}.
   688	     */
   689	    private boolean drainPendingDisconnects() {
   690	        boolean madeProgress = false;
   691	        String disconnectId;
   692	        while ((disconnectId = pendingDisconnects.poll()) != null) {
   693	            KafkaChannel channel = channels.remove(disconnectId);
   694	            nettyChannels.remove(disconnectId);
   695	            lastActiveNanos.remove(disconnectId);
   696	            if (channel == null) continue;
   697	            if (justAccepted.contains(disconnectId)) {
   698	                connected.remove(disconnectId);
   699	                Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "same-poll accept+disconnect, quota never inc'd");
   700	                madeProgress = true;
   701	                continue;
   702	            }
   703	            if (!receivesThisPoll.contains(disconnectId) && !channel.isMuted()) {
   704	                try {
   705	                    while (channel.ready()) {
   706	                        long read = channel.read();
   707	                        NetworkReceive completed = channel.maybeCompleteReceive();
   708	                        if (completed != null) {
   709	                            completedReceives.add(completed);
   710	                            receivesThisPoll.add(disconnectId);
   711	                            madeProgress = true;
   712	                            break;
   713	                        }
   714	                        if (read <= 0) break;
   715	                    }
   716	                } catch (Exception e) {
   717	                    // Catch Exception, not just IOException: InvalidReceiveException
   718	                    // (RuntimeException) during the FIN-drain must not escape — the
   719	                    // channel still needs to land in closingChannels for the eviction
   720	                    // notification path; otherwise the disconnect notification is lost.
   721	                    log.debug("Final read on disconnecting channel {} failed", disconnectId, e);
   722	                }
   723	            }
   724	            closingChannels.put(disconnectId, channel);
   725	            madeProgress = true;
   726	        }
   727	        return madeProgress;
   728	    }
   729	
   730	    private void acquireWithTimeout(long timeoutMs) {
   731	        try {
   732	            if (wakeup.tryAcquire(timeoutMs, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)) {
   733	                wakeup.drainPermits();
   734	            }
   735	        } catch (InterruptedException ie) {
   736	            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
   737	        }
   738	    }
   739	
   740	    private void enqueueClose(String id, ChannelState state) {
   741	        KafkaChannel channel = channels.get(id);
   742	        if (channel != null) {
   743	            nettyChannels.remove(id);
   744	            explicitlyMutedChannels.remove(channel);
   745	            disconnected.put(id, state);
   746	            Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "channel after I/O error");
   747	        }
   748	    }
   749	
   750	    @Override
   751	    public void wakeup() {
   752	        wakeup.release();
   753	    }
   754	
   755	    @Override
   756	    public void send(NetworkSend send) {
   757	        String destinationId = send.destinationId();
   758	        KafkaChannel channel = channels.get(destinationId);
   759	        if (channel == null) {
   760	            if (closingChannels.containsKey(destinationId)) {
   761	                // Peer FIN'd between the poll that produced this response and now. NIO
   762	                // (clients/.../Selector.java:391-413) routes this to failedSends rather
   763	                // than throwing: the original disconnect notification already fired in
   764	                // the previous poll, so on the next poll the closingChannels eviction
   765	                // will drop this id from failedSends and the duplicate is suppressed.
   766	                // Throwing here would propagate up through Processor.processChannelException
   767	                // → Processor.close → connectionQuotas.dec on a connection that was
   768	                // already dec'd by processDisconnected, walking the per-IP counter
   769	                // negative on every disconnect-races-response.
   770	                failedSends.add(destinationId);
   771	                return;
   772	            }
   773	            throw new IllegalStateException("Attempt to send on unknown channel " + destinationId);
   774	        }
   775	        try {
   776	            channel.setSend(send);
   777	        } catch (Exception e) {
   778	            // setSend throws if a send is already in progress — a Processor invariant
   779	            // violation rather than a network failure, but mirror NIO's behavior:
   780	            // surface as FAILED_SEND via the standard disconnected pipeline, close the
   781	            // channel, and re-throw so the bug is not silently swallowed. We are on the
   782	            // Processor thread between polls, so dropping the channel here is safe —
   783	            // there is no concurrent step-2 iterator to invalidate. We do NOT add to
   784	            // `disconnected` directly: the failedSends drain at the top of the next
   785	            // poll is the single source of truth for this notification, and the
   786	            // channel is no longer in channels/closingChannels so Processor's
   787	            // openOrClosingChannel(id) returns None and processChannelException's
   788	            // close(id) branch is skipped — no double dec.
   789	            log.error("Unexpected exception during setSend, closing connection {} and rethrowing", destinationId, e);
   790	            nettyChannels.remove(destinationId);
   791	            lastActiveNanos.remove(destinationId);
   792	            channels.remove(destinationId);
   793	            failedSends.add(destinationId);
   794	            Utils.closeQuietly(channel, "channel after setSend exception");
   795	            throw e;
   796	        }
   797	    }
   798	
   799	    @Override
   800	    public void mute(String id) {
   801	        // Route through the same-package bridge so KafkaChannel's state machine actually
   802	        // transitions to MUTED. SocketServer.scala calls selector.mute(id) immediately
   803	        // followed by channel.handleChannelMuteEvent(REQUEST_RECEIVED), which transitions
   804	        // MUTED → MUTED_AND_RESPONSE_PENDING — if we don't put the channel into MUTED
   805	        // here, that handleChannelMuteEvent call throws IllegalStateException and the
   806	        // first valid request on every connection crashes. KafkaChannel.mute() also
   807	        // calls transport.removeInterestOps(OP_READ), which our IoUringTransportLayer
   808	        // override translates into Netty autoRead=false, so kernel-level backpressure
   809	        // happens atomically with the state transition.
   810	        KafkaChannel channel = channels.get(id);
   811	        if (channel == null) channel = closingChannels.get(id);
   812	        if (channel == null) return;
   813	        KafkaChannelMuteBridge.mute(channel);
   814	        // Track operator-driven mutes separately from self-mutes due to memory pressure.
   815	        // The recovery loop at the top of poll() uses this set to decide which channels
   816	        // are safe to unmute when memory pressure clears.
   817	        explicitlyMutedChannels.add(channel);
   818	    }
   819	
   820	    @Override
   821	    public void unmute(String id) {
   822	        KafkaChannel channel = channels.get(id);
   823	        if (channel == null) channel = closingChannels.get(id);
   824	        if (channel == null) return;
   825	        if (KafkaChannelMuteBridge.maybeUnmute(channel)) {
   826	            // Drop from the operator-muted set only on successful unmute. NIO does the
   827	            // same (Selector.unmute lines 762-763): if maybeUnmute returns false (e.g.
   828	            // the channel is in MUTED_AND_RESPONSE_PENDING and not yet ready to leave
   829	            // MUTED), the set entry stays so the next unmute attempt still respects the
   830	            // operator intent.
   831	            explicitlyMutedChannels.remove(channel);
   832	            // unmute may have flipped autoRead on; bytes may now flow into the transport
   833	            // queue and the next read step needs to drain them, so wake any blocking poll.
   834	            wakeup.release();
   835	        }
   836	    }
   837	
   838	    @Override
   839	    public void muteAll() {
   840	        for (KafkaChannel channel : channels.values()) {
   841	            KafkaChannelMuteBridge.mute(channel);
   842	            explicitlyMutedChannels.add(channel);
   843	        }
   844	    }
   845	
   846	    @Override
   847	    public void unmuteAll() {
   848	        for (KafkaChannel channel : channels.values()) {
   849	            if (KafkaChannelMuteBridge.maybeUnmute(channel)) {
   850	                explicitlyMutedChannels.remove(channel);
   851	            }
   852	        }
   853	        wakeup.release();
   854	    }
   855	
   856	    @Override
   857	    public boolean isChannelReady(String id) {
   858	        KafkaChannel channel = channels.get(id);
   859	        return channel != null && channel.ready();
   860	    }

codex
The core selector keeps the expected NIO-style structures: active channels, closing channels, per-poll outputs, pending accept/disconnect queues, and explicit mute tracking. I’m now checking the transport and listener where direct memory, Netty wakeups, and OS socket options are most likely to diverge under load.

    17	package org.apache.kafka.network.iouring;
    18	
    19	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.TransportLayer;
    20	import org.apache.kafka.common.security.auth.KafkaPrincipal;
    21	
    22	import java.io.IOException;
    23	import java.net.InetSocketAddress;
    24	import java.nio.ByteBuffer;
    25	import java.nio.channels.FileChannel;
    26	import java.nio.channels.SelectionKey;
    27	import java.nio.channels.SocketChannel;
    28	import java.security.Principal;
    29	import java.util.Queue;
    30	import java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentLinkedQueue;
    31	import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicLong;
    32	
    33	import io.netty.buffer.ByteBuf;
    34	import io.netty.channel.Channel;
    35	
    36	/**
    37	 * {@link TransportLayer} that bridges Kafka's {@code KafkaChannel} to a Netty
    38	 * {@code IoUringSocketChannel} (or, in tests, any Netty {@code Channel}).
    39	 *
    40	 * <p>Two threads touch this object:
    41	 * <ul>
    42	 *   <li>The <strong>Processor thread</strong> calls {@code read}, {@code write},
    43	 *       {@code disconnect}, {@code close}, {@code hasBytesBuffered}, the interest-ops
    44	 *       methods, and the various getters.</li>
    45	 *   <li>The <strong>Netty event-loop thread</strong> calls {@link #offerInbound(ByteBuf)}
    46	 *       on every {@code channelRead} and {@link #markEof()} on {@code channelInactive}.
    47	 *       It must never call into the read/write/close methods.</li>
    48	 * </ul>
    49	 *
    50	 * <p>Cross-thread state lives in a {@link ConcurrentLinkedQueue} of inbound
    51	 * {@link ByteBuf}s plus two {@code volatile} flags ({@code eofSeen}, {@code closed}).
    52	 * Outbound writes call {@link Channel#writeAndFlush(Object)}, which Netty documents as
    53	 * safe to call from any thread — internally it schedules onto the channel's own event
    54	 * loop.
    55	 *
    56	 * <p>The interest-ops methods are no-ops at the kernel layer. Kafka's {@code KafkaChannel}
    57	 * updates the interest mask to signal "I want OP_READ" / "I want OP_WRITE" — under NIO
    58	 * that registers with the JDK Selector; here io_uring drives reads autonomously
    59	 * (via Netty's autoRead) and writes via {@link Channel#writeAndFlush(Object)}, so the
    60	 * interest mask is purely advisory. {@link #isMute()} still derives from it, exactly as
    61	 * {@code PlaintextTransportLayer} does, so {@code KafkaChannel.isMuted()} matches the
    62	 * NIO baseline.
    63	 */
    64	final class IoUringTransportLayer implements TransportLayer {
    65	
    66	    /**
    67	     * Hard upper bound on the number of bytes a single {@link #write(ByteBuffer)} call will
    68	     * allocate in one direct {@link ByteBuf}. Two failure modes are bounded by this cap:
    69	     *
    70	     * <ol>
    71	     *   <li><b>Direct-memory OOM under a single huge response.</b> A 100 MiB Fetch response
    72	     *       reaching a channel whose Netty outbound buffer is empty would otherwise allocate
    73	     *       a 100 MiB direct buffer in one shot — bypassing the high-water-mark gate, which
    74	     *       only flips after the queue is already past the limit. Capping each call to 1 MiB
    75	     *       keeps the per-call footprint bounded; the source ByteBuffer keeps its remainder
    76	     *       and ByteBufferSend re-enters on the next poll, mirroring how NIO returns partial
    77	     *       progress when {@code SO_SNDBUF} saturates.</li>
    78	     *   <li><b>Direct-memory exhaustion across many slow consumers.</b> Without the cap,
    79	     *       N concurrent in-flight big sends each allocate N * responseSize. Even with the
    80	     *       Netty watermark gate, a burst of slow consumers can collectively exceed direct
    81	     *       memory before any single channel trips the gate. The cap bounds the
    82	     *       worst-case per-call allocation, regardless of how many channels are writing.</li>
    83	     * </ol>
    84	     *
    85	     * <p>1 MiB is a deliberate balance: small enough to keep direct memory bounded under DoS,
    86	     * large enough that typical Kafka response sizes (Produce ack, Fetch chunk) drain in a
    87	     * couple of poll iterations rather than thousands.
    88	     */
    89	    static final int MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES = 1 << 20;
    90	
    91	    /**
    92	     * High-water mark for the per-channel inbound ByteBuf queue. When {@link #inboundBytes}
    93	     * exceeds this, {@link #offerInbound(ByteBuf)} flips Netty {@code autoRead} off so the
    94	     * kernel applies TCP-level backpressure to the peer. Without this gate, a fast malicious
    95	     * PLAINTEXT client (or simply a slow Processor) lets ByteBufs accumulate unbounded in
    96	     * the transport queue — direct memory exhaustion takes the broker down before the
    97	     * KafkaChannel-level MemoryPool gate (queued.max.bytes) even kicks in, because the
    98	     * MemoryPool only governs the destination NetworkReceive buffer, not the raw bytes
    99	     * still riding in Netty's inbound side.
   100	     */
   101	    static final int INBOUND_HIGH_WATERMARK_BYTES = 1 << 20;
   102	
   103	    /**
   104	     * Low-water mark for the per-channel inbound ByteBuf queue. Once {@link #inboundBytes}
   105	     * drops below this after a Processor drain, {@link #read} flips Netty {@code autoRead}
   106	     * back on so kernel reads resume. The HIGH-LOW gap prevents oscillation: a single
   107	     * fluctuation around the high water mark would otherwise trigger constant autoRead
   108	     * flapping. Only re-enabled when the channel is not muted ({@link #isMute()}) — an
   109	     * operator mute or memory-pool self-mute must keep autoRead off regardless of queue
   110	     * depth, otherwise the request-pipeline throttle is bypassed.
   111	     */
   112	    static final int INBOUND_LOW_WATERMARK_BYTES = 1 << 18;
   113	
   114	    private final Channel nettyChannel;
   115	    private final StubSocketChannel socketChannel;
   116	    private final NoopSelectionKey selectionKey;
   117	
   118	    private final Queue<ByteBuf> inbound = new ConcurrentLinkedQueue<>();
   119	    /**
   120	     * Total readable bytes currently sitting in {@link #inbound}. Bumped from the event-loop
   121	     * thread inside {@link #offerInbound}, drained from the Processor thread inside
   122	     * {@link #read}. Drives the autoRead watermark gate so a slow Processor (or fast/abusive
   123	     * peer) cannot accumulate unbounded direct memory in the transport queue.
   124	     *
   125	     * <p>The counter is also decremented in {@link #close()} when remaining ByteBufs are
   126	     * released, so a teardown does not leave the counter falsely positive should the
   127	     * channel be re-used (it is not, in v1, but the invariant is cheap to maintain).
   128	     */
   129	    private final AtomicLong inboundBytes = new AtomicLong(0);
   130	    private volatile boolean eofSeen;
   131	    private volatile boolean closed;
   132	    /**
   133	     * Bytes still riding inside Netty's outbound buffer for this channel — incremented on
   134	     * write(), decremented on writeAndFlush completion. {@link #hasPendingWrites()} reads
   135	     * this so {@code KafkaChannel.maybeCompleteSend} only reports "send done" once the
   136	     * bytes have actually been handed off to the kernel by Netty, not just queued.
   137	     */
   138	    private final AtomicLong pendingWriteBytes = new AtomicLong(0);
   139	    /**
   140	     * Async write failure surfaced by Netty's writeAndFlush promise. If the kernel rejected
   141	     * the write (peer RST mid-response, kernel buffer pressure, channel closed under us),
   142	     * Netty calls our listener with {@code !future.isSuccess()}; we cannot route the failure
   143	     * back to the Processor inline because the listener runs on the event loop. Instead we
   144	     * stash the cause here and re-throw it from the next {@link #write(ByteBuffer)} call.
   145	     * That converts the silent async failure into a synchronous {@link IOException} that
   146	     * {@link IoUringSelector#poll(long)}'s write step catches and routes through
   147	     * {@code ChannelState.FAILED_SEND} — matching how NIO surfaces a peer-RST'd write
   148	     * through its {@code Selector.poll} loop.
   149	     *
   150	     * <p>Volatile so the Processor sees the latest cause without an explicit memory barrier:
   151	     * the only writer is the event-loop listener thread, the only reader is the Processor
   152	     * thread, and a {@code happens-before} relationship is not strictly required (we re-check
   153	     * each call), but volatile keeps reasoning simple.
   154	     */
   155	    private volatile Throwable asyncWriteFailure;
   156	
   157	    /**
   158	     * Wakes the owning {@link IoUringSelector}'s blocking {@code poll(timeoutMs)} when the
   159	     * Netty writeAndFlush promise completes async on the event-loop thread. Without this,
   160	     * the listener decrements {@link #pendingWriteBytes} silently — the Processor thread
   161	     * stays asleep on {@code wakeup.acquireWithTimeout} until the next event (300 ms by
   162	     * default) and {@code completedSends}/{@code RESPONSE_SENT} are delayed by that whole
   163	     * window for every small response. {@code onWritabilityChanged} only fires when the
   164	     * outbound buffer crosses high/low water marks, so a typical sub-watermark response
   165	     * (e.g. 1 KB) never triggers it. We therefore call this Runnable from inside the
   166	     * writeAndFlush listener, after {@link #pendingWriteBytes} is decremented and after
   167	     * any {@link #asyncWriteFailure} is recorded.
   168	     *
   169	     * <p>EmbeddedChannel completes writeAndFlush synchronously on the calling thread, so
   170	     * unit tests using EmbeddedChannel never expose the stall this callback prevents;
   171	     * see {@code asyncWriteCompletionWakesSelector} for the regression test that uses an
   172	     * out-of-loop completion to actually exercise the async path.
   173	     */
   174	    private final Runnable writeWakeCallback;
   175	
   176	    IoUringTransportLayer(Channel nettyChannel, InetSocketAddress remote, InetSocketAddress local) {
   177	        this(nettyChannel, remote, local, () -> { });
   178	    }
   179	
   180	    IoUringTransportLayer(Channel nettyChannel, InetSocketAddress remote, InetSocketAddress local,
   181	                          Runnable writeWakeCallback) {
   182	        this.nettyChannel = nettyChannel;
   183	        this.socketChannel = new StubSocketChannel(remote, local);
   184	        this.selectionKey = new NoopSelectionKey();
   185	        this.writeWakeCallback = writeWakeCallback;
   186	        // Match Selector.register: freshly registered channels start with OP_READ set so
   187	        // isMute() reports false. io_uring's autoRead=true is the equivalent of OP_READ.
   188	        this.selectionKey.interestOps(SelectionKey.OP_READ);
   189	    }
   190	
   191	    /**
   192	     * Called from the Netty event-loop thread on every {@code channelRead}. The buffer
   193	     * is consumed by subsequent {@link #read(ByteBuffer)} calls on the Processor thread.
   194	     *
   195	     * <p>The closed-check is repeated <em>after</em> the offer to close a leak window
   196	     * with the Processor's {@link #close()}: if Processor sets {@code closed=true} and
   197	     * drains the queue in between our read of {@code closed} and our {@code offer()},
   198	     * the buf we just queued would never be released. Re-checking and draining ourselves
   199	     * keeps the contract that every {@code offerInbound} either delivers the buf or
   200	     * releases it.
   201	     */
   202	    void offerInbound(ByteBuf buf) {
   203	        if (closed) {
   204	            buf.release();
   205	            return;
   206	        }
   207	        int size = buf.readableBytes();
   208	        inbound.offer(buf);
   209	        if (closed) {
   210	            // Race with Processor's close(): drain anything we just queued so we don't leak.
   211	            ByteBuf b;
   212	            while ((b = inbound.poll()) != null) {
   213	                b.release();
   214	            }
   215	            inboundBytes.set(0);
   216	            return;
   217	        }
   218	        long after = inboundBytes.addAndGet(size);
   219	        // Inbound watermark gate. When the queue crosses the high water mark, flip Netty
   220	        // autoRead off so the kernel stops pushing more bytes to us — TCP's own flow
   221	        // control will throttle the peer. Without this gate, a fast/abusive PLAINTEXT
   222	        // client (or simply a slow Processor) lets ByteBufs accumulate unbounded in the
   223	        // inbound queue; direct-memory OOM takes the broker down before KafkaChannel's
   224	        // MemoryPool-based queued.max.bytes throttle even fires (that throttle governs
   225	        // the destination NetworkReceive buffer, not the bytes still sitting in this
   226	        // queue waiting to be drained).
   227	        //
   228	        // We always defer the autoRead=false to the channel's own event loop, even though
   229	        // offerInbound is invoked from the event loop in production. EmbeddedChannel in
   230	        // tests routes channelRead synchronously on the caller's thread — calling
   231	        // setAutoRead inline on the test path could deadlock if it tried to re-enter the
   232	        // pipeline. The event loop is always the right thread to flip Netty config.
   233	        if (after >= INBOUND_HIGH_WATERMARK_BYTES && nettyChannel.config().isAutoRead()) {
   234	            nettyChannel.config().setAutoRead(false);
   235	        }
   236	    }
   237	
   238	    /**
   239	     * Called from the Netty event-loop thread on {@code channelInactive}. Subsequent
   240	     * {@link #read(ByteBuffer)} calls drain whatever is still queued, then start
   241	     * returning {@code -1}.
   242	     */
   243	    void markEof() {
   244	        eofSeen = true;
   245	    }
   246	
   247	    @Override
   248	    public boolean ready() {
   249	        return true;
   250	    }
   251	
   252	    @Override
   253	    public boolean finishConnect() {
   254	        return true;
   255	    }
   256	
   257	    @Override
   258	    public void disconnect() {
   259	        close();
   260	    }

   262	    @Override
   263	    public boolean isConnected() {
   264	        return !closed && nettyChannel.isActive();
   265	    }

   267	    @Override
   268	    public SocketChannel socketChannel() {
   269	        return socketChannel;
   270	    }
   271	
   272	    @Override
   273	    public SelectionKey selectionKey() {
   274	        return selectionKey;
   275	    }
   276	
   277	    @Override
   278	    public void handshake() {
   279	        // PLAINTEXT: nothing to do.
   280	    }
   281	
   282	    @Override
   283	    public Principal peerPrincipal() {
   284	        return KafkaPrincipal.ANONYMOUS;
   285	    }
   286	
   287	    @Override
   288	    public void addInterestOps(int ops) {
   289	        int before = selectionKey.interestOps();
   290	        int after = before | ops;
   291	        selectionKey.interestOps(after);
   292	        // Mirror the NIO contract: when OP_READ comes back in the interest mask the channel
   293	        // is once again accepting reads. Flip Netty autoRead on so the kernel resumes pushing
   294	        // bytes. This is what KafkaChannel.maybeUnmute() relies on after MemoryPool pressure
   295	        // releases, and it is also the symmetric counterpart of removeInterestOps below.
   296	        if ((after & SelectionKey.OP_READ) != 0 && (before & SelectionKey.OP_READ) == 0
   297	                && nettyChannel.isOpen() && !nettyChannel.config().isAutoRead()) {
   298	            nettyChannel.config().setAutoRead(true);
   299	        }
   300	    }
   301	
   302	    @Override
   303	    public void removeInterestOps(int ops) {
   304	        int before = selectionKey.interestOps();
   305	        int after = before & ~ops;
   306	        selectionKey.interestOps(after);
   307	        // On the NIO path, removing OP_READ takes the SocketChannel out of the JDK selector's
   308	        // ready set so the broker stops draining the kernel buffer. On io_uring there is no
   309	        // SelectionKey driving the kernel — Netty's autoRead does. Reflect the mute side-effect
   310	        // here so KafkaChannel.mute() (called internally when MemoryPool.tryAllocate returns
   311	        // null) actually applies kernel-level backpressure instead of letting bytes pile up
   312	        // in the transport's inbound queue.
   313	        if ((after & SelectionKey.OP_READ) == 0 && (before & SelectionKey.OP_READ) != 0
   314	                && nettyChannel.isOpen() && nettyChannel.config().isAutoRead()) {
   315	            nettyChannel.config().setAutoRead(false);
   316	        }
   317	    }
   318	
   319	    @Override
   320	    public boolean isMute() {
   321	        return (selectionKey.interestOps() & SelectionKey.OP_READ) == 0;
   322	    }
   323	
   324	    @Override
   325	    public boolean hasBytesBuffered() {
   326	        return !inbound.isEmpty();
   327	    }
   328	
   329	    @Override
   330	    public int read(ByteBuffer dst) throws IOException {
   331	        if (closed) throw new IOException("transport layer is closed");
   332	        int total = 0;
   333	        while (dst.hasRemaining()) {
   334	            ByteBuf head = inbound.peek();
   335	            if (head == null) break;
   336	            int toRead = Math.min(head.readableBytes(), dst.remaining());
   337	            // Bound the destination so head.readBytes never over-reads — Netty's
   338	            // readBytes(ByteBuffer) keeps going until dst is full.
   339	            int savedLimit = dst.limit();
   340	            dst.limit(dst.position() + toRead);
   341	            head.readBytes(dst);
   342	            dst.limit(savedLimit);
   343	            total += toRead;
   344	            if (!head.isReadable()) {
   345	                inbound.poll();
   346	                head.release();
   347	            }
   348	        }
   349	        if (total > 0) {
   350	            // Inbound watermark gate (read-side): once the Processor has drained enough
   351	            // bytes for the queue to fall below the LOW water mark, flip autoRead back on
   352	            // so the kernel resumes pushing bytes. Only re-enable on an unmuted channel —
   353	            // an operator mute (RESPONSE_QUEUED throttling) or memory-pool self-mute
   354	            // (KafkaChannel.read failed to allocate) must keep autoRead off regardless of
   355	            // queue depth, otherwise the request-pipeline throttle is bypassed. We re-
   356	            // check the autoRead flag itself so we don't fight a state legitimately set
   357	            // by addInterestOps/removeInterestOps — only flip when we know we lowered it
   358	            // due to backpressure.
   359	            long after = inboundBytes.addAndGet(-total);
   360	            if (after <= INBOUND_LOW_WATERMARK_BYTES
   361	                    && !isMute()
   362	                    && nettyChannel.isOpen()
   363	                    && !nettyChannel.config().isAutoRead()) {
   364	                nettyChannel.config().setAutoRead(true);
   365	            }
   366	        }
   367	        if (total == 0 && eofSeen && inbound.isEmpty()) return -1;
   368	        return total;
   369	    }
   370	
   371	    @Override
   372	    public long read(ByteBuffer[] dsts, int offset, int length) throws IOException {
   373	        long total = 0;
   374	        boolean anyProgress = false;
   375	        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {
   376	            ByteBuffer dst = dsts[offset + i];
   377	            if (!dst.hasRemaining()) continue;
   378	            int n = read(dst);
   379	            if (n == -1) {
   380	                return anyProgress ? total : -1;
   381	            }
   382	            if (n == 0) break;
   383	            total += n;
   384	            anyProgress = true;
   385	        }
   386	        return total;
   387	    }
   388	
   389	    @Override
   390	    public long read(ByteBuffer[] dsts) throws IOException {
   391	        return read(dsts, 0, dsts.length);
   392	    }
   393	
   394	    @Override
   395	    public int write(ByteBuffer src) throws IOException {
   396	        if (closed) throw new IOException("transport layer is closed");
   397	        // If a prior async write already failed, surface it now so the Processor's write
   398	        // step routes the channel through FAILED_SEND rather than reporting completedSend
   399	        // for bytes the kernel never delivered.
   400	        Throwable failure = asyncWriteFailure;
   401	        if (failure != null) {
   402	            asyncWriteFailure = null;
   403	            throw new IOException("async write failed", failure);
   404	        }
   405	        int remaining = src.remaining();
   406	        if (remaining == 0) return 0;
   407	        // Backpressure + bounded allocation. Two failure modes have to be defended here:
   408	        //
   409	        //   (a) The classic "Netty outbound buffer past high water mark" case: a slow peer
   410	        //       isn't draining fast enough, isWritable() is false, queueing more bytes
   411	        //       would bloat direct memory until the broker OOMs. Return 0; the next
   412	        //       Selector poll re-attempts, and onWritabilityChanged() wakes the poll the
   413	        //       moment the buffer drains below the low water mark.
   414	        //
   415	        //   (b) The "huge response in one shot" case: a 100MB Fetch response arrives at a
   416	        //       channel whose outbound buffer is currently empty — isWritable() is true.
   417	        //       The naive implementation allocates a 100MB direct ByteBuf in a single call
   418	        //       BEFORE the high water mark gate kicks in. Many slow consumers in flight at
   419	        //       once can each spike 100MB of direct memory, bypassing the gate entirely.
   420	        //       The fix is to cap each write to {@code bytesBeforeUnwritable()} (Netty's
   421	        //       remaining headroom under the high water mark) AND to a hard
   422	        //       {@link #MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES} chunk so a single call cannot drown the
   423	        //       pooled allocator. ByteBufferSend re-enters write() on the next poll and the
   424	        //       source ByteBuffer keeps its remainder, so correctness for large responses
   425	        //       is preserved — only the per-call allocation footprint changes.
   426	        //
   427	        // Restrict (a) to healthy channels (isActive() = open + connected). For a closed/
   428	        // disconnected channel, isWritable() is false too — but we WANT those writes to
   429	        // proceed so writeAndFlush's promise fails and surfaces the error through
   430	        // asyncWriteFailure. Returning 0 on a dead channel would silently swallow the
   431	        // error: ByteBufferSend.writeTo would loop forever waiting for writability that
   432	        // never comes, while the Send is silently considered "in flight".
   433	        long room;
   434	        if (nettyChannel.isActive()) {
   435	            room = nettyChannel.bytesBeforeUnwritable();
   436	            if (room <= 0) return 0;
   437	        } else {
   438	            // Channel already torn down — bypass the watermark gate so the writeAndFlush
   439	            // failure path surfaces. Cap by MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES regardless: even on a
   440	            // dying channel we don't want to over-allocate before the failure surfaces.
   441	            room = MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES;
   442	        }
   443	        int safeChunk = (int) Math.min((long) remaining, Math.min(room, (long) MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES));
   444	        // Use a pooled direct buffer so io_uring can submit the bytes without a heap-to-
   445	        // direct intermediate copy. The buffer is released by Netty after the channel has
   446	        // flushed it; we only own the writeAndFlush completion listener.
   447	        ByteBuf buf = nettyChannel.alloc().directBuffer(safeChunk);
   448	        // Anything between allocation and writeAndFlush taking ownership must release the
   449	        // buf on failure — otherwise the pooled allocator slowly bleeds direct memory.
   450	        boolean handedOff = false;
   451	        try {
   452	            // Bound src so writeBytes only consumes safeChunk bytes. ByteBufferSend.writeTo
   453	            // re-enters on the next poll with the remainder, mirroring how NIO's
   454	            // SocketChannel.write returns partial progress when SO_SNDBUF saturates.
   455	            int savedLimit = src.limit();
   456	            src.limit(src.position() + safeChunk);
   457	            buf.writeBytes(src);
   458	            src.limit(savedLimit);
   459	            // Install the dec listener BEFORE incrementing pendingWriteBytes. If the
   460	            // listener registration itself throws synchronously (DefaultPromise can
   461	            // throw when the executor is shut down), inc'ing first would strand the
   462	            // counter positive — hasPendingWrites() then permanently reports true and
   463	            // KafkaChannel.maybeCompleteSend never advances. The ByteBuf itself is
   464	            // released by writeAndFlush's promise on either path (success or failure),
   465	            // so the only leak we have to defend against here is the counter.
   466	            io.netty.channel.ChannelFuture future = nettyChannel.writeAndFlush(buf);
   467	            handedOff = true;
   468	            future.addListener(f -> {
   469	                pendingWriteBytes.addAndGet(-safeChunk);
   470	                if (!f.isSuccess()) {
   471	                    // Record the cause so the next Processor write step can throw it
   472	                    // synchronously and route the channel through FAILED_SEND. Without
   473	                    // this, a peer RST mid-response leaves the broker thinking the send
   474	                    // completed normally — completedSends fires, RESPONSE_SENT mute event
   475	                    // succeeds, and the request handling pipeline silently advances on a
   476	                    // request the client never saw.
   477	                    asyncWriteFailure = f.cause();
   478	                }
   479	                // Wake the Processor's poll(). The listener runs on Netty's event loop
   480	                // thread (a separate thread from the Processor in production), so without
   481	                // this callback the Processor stays asleep on its wakeup Semaphore until
   482	                // the poll timeout expires — even though hasPendingWrites() now reads
   483	                // false and maybeCompleteSend() would return the completed Send on the
   484	                // very next poll iteration. For a small response that never crosses the
   485	                // outbound watermark, channelWritabilityChanged is not invoked, so no
   486	                // other wakeup source exists. Result without this line: every small
   487	                // request/response incurs an extra ~300 ms (the default poll timeout)
   488	                // before the broker emits RESPONSE_SENT. The bug is invisible under
   489	                // EmbeddedChannel because that channel completes writeAndFlush futures
   490	                // synchronously on the calling thread.
   491	                writeWakeCallback.run();
   492	            });
   493	            pendingWriteBytes.addAndGet(safeChunk);
   494	        } finally {
   495	            if (!handedOff) {
   496	                buf.release();
   497	            }
   498	        }
   499	        return safeChunk;
   500	    }
   501	
   502	    @Override
   503	    public long write(ByteBuffer[] srcs, int offset, int length) throws IOException {
   504	        // Check the async failure FIRST, before scanning for buffers with bytes — a
   505	        // failed prior write must surface even when the Send has fully drained its
   506	        // ByteBuffers and the inner write(ByteBuffer) call would otherwise be skipped.
   507	        // ByteBufferSend.writeTo calls into this overload, so this is the actual fault
   508	        // path that converts the volatile failure into the synchronous IOException the
   509	        // Selector's write step needs to route the channel through FAILED_SEND.
   510	        if (closed) throw new IOException("transport layer is closed");
   511	        Throwable failure = asyncWriteFailure;
   512	        if (failure != null) {
   513	            asyncWriteFailure = null;
   514	            throw new IOException("async write failed", failure);
   515	        }
   516	        long total = 0;
   517	        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {
   518	            ByteBuffer src = srcs[offset + i];
   519	            if (!src.hasRemaining()) continue;
   520	            // GatheringByteChannel ordering: a partial write of {@code src} (because of
   521	            // {@link #MAX_WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES} or {@code bytesBeforeUnwritable} pressure) must
   522	            // halt the loop here. Continuing to a later buffer would interleave its bytes
   523	            // ahead of the remainder of {@code src}, scrambling on-wire framing for any
   524	            // multi-buffer {@link org.apache.kafka.common.network.ByteBufferSend} —
   525	            // notably Fetch responses whose Send is built as
   526	            // {@code [header, recordSet, trailer]}: a partial write of {@code recordSet}
   527	            // followed by writes from {@code trailer} would emit trailer bytes inside
   528	            // the record set. {@link java.nio.channels.SocketChannel#write(ByteBuffer[])}
   529	            // never reorders, and our impl must match. A zero return (backpressured)
   530	            // similarly aborts the loop so the caller re-enters on the next poll.
   531	            int wrote = write(src);
   532	            total += wrote;
   533	            if (wrote == 0 || src.hasRemaining()) {
   534	                break;
   535	            }
   536	        }
   537	        return total;
   538	    }
   539	
   540	    @Override
   541	    public long write(ByteBuffer[] srcs) throws IOException {
   542	        return write(srcs, 0, srcs.length);
   543	    }
   544	
   545	    @Override
   546	    public boolean hasPendingWrites() {
   547	        // Use our own counter, not nettyChannel.isWritable(): the latter only flips when
   548	        // the outbound queue crosses Netty's high water mark, so any send below the mark
   549	        // would otherwise report "fully drained" the instant write() returned — even though
   550	        // the bytes are still sitting in Netty's queue waiting for the event loop to flush.
   551	        //
   552	        // Also report pending when a prior write failed asynchronously: ByteBufferSend
   553	        // calls hasPendingWrites() inside writeTo() to decide if the Send is completed,
   554	        // and a synchronous-listener failure could otherwise zero the counter before the
   555	        // next Processor write call surfaces the throw. Holding "pending" until the next
   556	        // write() throws keeps {@code Send.completed} false in that interleaving, so
   557	        // {@code KafkaChannel.maybeCompleteSend} does NOT fire on the failed bytes.
   558	        return pendingWriteBytes.get() > 0 || asyncWriteFailure != null;
   559	    }
   560	
   561	    @Override
   562	    public long transferFrom(FileChannel fileChannel, long position, long count) throws IOException {
   563	        // Naive read-then-write. v1 does not chase the zero-copy fast path; the broker
   564	        // serves PLAINTEXT log segments through the same code path as any other write,
   565	        // so correctness comes first.
   566	        int chunk = (int) Math.min(count, 64 * 1024);
   567	        ByteBuffer buf = ByteBuffer.allocate(chunk);
   568	        int n = fileChannel.read(buf, position);
   569	        if (n <= 0) return 0;
   570	        buf.flip();
   571	        return write(buf);
   572	    }
   573	
   574	    @Override
   575	    public boolean isOpen() {
   576	        return !closed;
   577	    }
   578	
   579	    @Override
   580	    public void close() {
   581	        if (closed) return;
   582	        closed = true;
   583	        try {
   584	            if (nettyChannel.isOpen()) nettyChannel.close();
   585	        } catch (Exception ignored) { /* close is best-effort */ }
   586	        ByteBuf b;
   587	        while ((b = inbound.poll()) != null) {
   588	            b.release();
   589	        }
   590	        inboundBytes.set(0);
   591	        selectionKey.cancel();
   592	    }
   593	}
