 succeeded in 0ms:
  1815	  /**
  1816	   * Calculates the delay needed to bring the observed connection creation rate to the IP limit.
  1817	   * If the connection would cause an IP quota violation, un-record the connection for both IP,
  1818	   * listener, and broker connection rate and throw a ConnectionThrottledException. Calls to
  1819	   * this function must be performed with the counts lock to ensure that reading the IP
  1820	   * connection rate quota and creating the sensor's metric config is atomic.
  1821	   *
  1822	   * @param listenerName listener to unrecord connection if throttled
  1823	   * @param address ip address to record connection
  1824	   */
  1825	  private def recordIpConnectionMaybeThrottle(listenerName: ListenerName, address: InetAddress): Unit = {
  1826	    val connectionRateQuota = connectionRateForIp(address)
  1827	    val quotaEnabled = connectionRateQuota != QuotaConfig.IP_CONNECTION_RATE_DEFAULT
  1828	    if (quotaEnabled) {
  1829	      val sensor = getOrCreateConnectionRateQuotaSensor(connectionRateQuota, ConnectionQuotaEntity.ipQuotaEntity(address))
  1830	      val timeMs = time.milliseconds
  1831	      val throttleMs = recordAndGetThrottleTimeMs(sensor, timeMs)
  1832	      if (throttleMs > 0) {
  1833	        trace(s"Throttling $address for $throttleMs ms")
  1834	        // unrecord the connection since we won't accept the connection
  1835	        sensor.record(-1.0, timeMs, false)
  1836	        updateListenerMetrics(listenerName, throttleMs, timeMs)
  1837	        throw new ConnectionThrottledException(address, timeMs, throttleMs)
  1838	      }
  1839	    }
  1840	  }
  1841	
  1842	  /**
  1843	   * Records a new connection into a given connection acceptance rate sensor 'sensor' and returns throttle time
  1844	   * in milliseconds if quota got violated
  1845	   * @param sensor sensor to record connection
  1846	   * @param timeMs current time in milliseconds
  1847	   * @return throttle time in milliseconds if quota got violated, otherwise 0
  1848	   */
  1849	  private def recordAndGetThrottleTimeMs(sensor: Sensor, timeMs: Long): Int = {
  1850	    try {
  1851	      sensor.record(1.0, timeMs)
  1852	      0
  1853	    } catch {
  1854	      case e: QuotaViolationException =>
  1855	        val throttleTimeMs = QuotaUtils.boundedThrottleTime(e, maxThrottleTimeMs, timeMs).toInt
  1856	        debug(s"Quota violated for sensor (${sensor.name}). Delay time: $throttleTimeMs ms")
  1857	        throttleTimeMs
  1858	    }
  1859	  }
  1860	
  1861	  /**
  1862	   * Creates sensor for tracking the connection creation rate and corresponding connection rate quota for a given
  1863	   * listener or broker-wide, if listener is not provided.
  1864	   * @param quotaLimit connection creation rate quota
  1865	   * @param connectionQuotaEntity entity to create the sensor for
  1866	   */
  1867	  private def getOrCreateConnectionRateQuotaSensor(quotaLimit: Int, connectionQuotaEntity: ConnectionQuotaEntity): Sensor = {
  1868	    Option(metrics.getSensor(connectionQuotaEntity.sensorName)).getOrElse {
  1869	      val sensor = metrics.sensor(
  1870	        connectionQuotaEntity.sensorName,
  1871	        rateQuotaMetricConfig(quotaLimit),
  1872	        connectionQuotaEntity.sensorExpiration
  1873	      )
  1874	      sensor.add(connectionRateMetricName(connectionQuotaEntity), new Rate, null)
  1875	      sensor

    18	package org.apache.kafka.common.network;
    19	
    20	import java.net.Socket;
    21	import java.util.Map;
    22	import java.util.Optional;
    23	import java.util.regex.Matcher;
    24	import java.util.regex.Pattern;
    25	
    26	/**
    27	 * ServerConnectionId is used to uniquely identify a connection on server for the client. The
    28	 *  connection id is in the format of "localHost:localPort-remoteHost:remotePort-processorId-index".
    29	 *  The processorId is the id of the processor that will handle this connection and the index is
    30	 *  used to ensure uniqueness.
    31	 */
    32	public class ServerConnectionId {
    33	
    34	    // The regex for parsing the host:port string, where host can be an IPv4 address or an IPv6 address.
    35	    //  Note: The IPv6 address should not be enclosed in square brackets.
    36	    private static final Pattern HOST_PORT_PARSE_EXP = Pattern.compile("([0-9a-zA-Z\\-%._:]*):([0-9]+)");
    37	
    38	    private final String localHost;
    39	    private final int localPort;
    40	    private final String remoteHost;
    41	    private final int remotePort;
    42	    private final int processorId;
    43	    private final int index;
    44	
    45	    public ServerConnectionId(
    46	        String localHost,
    47	        int localPort,
    48	        String remoteHost,
    49	        int remotePort,
    50	        int processorId,
    51	        int index
    52	    ) {
    53	        this.localHost = localHost;
    54	        this.localPort = localPort;
    55	        this.remoteHost = remoteHost;
    56	        this.remotePort = remotePort;
    57	        this.processorId = processorId;
    58	        this.index = index;
    59	    }
    60	
    61	    private ServerConnectionId(
    62	        Map.Entry<String, Integer> localEndpoint,
    63	        Map.Entry<String, Integer> remoteEndpoint,
    64	        int processorId,
    65	        int index
    66	    ) {
    67	        this(localEndpoint.getKey(), localEndpoint.getValue(), remoteEndpoint.getKey(), remoteEndpoint.getValue(), processorId, index);
    68	    }
    69	
    70	    public String localHost() {
    71	        return localHost;
    72	    }
    73	
    74	    public int localPort() {
    75	        return localPort;
    76	    }
    77	
    78	    public String remoteHost() {
    79	        return remoteHost;
    80	    }
    81	
    82	    public int remotePort() {
    83	        return remotePort;
    84	    }
    85	
    86	    public int processorId() {
    87	        return processorId;
    88	    }
    89	
    90	    public int index() {
    91	        return index;
    92	    }
    93	
    94	    /**
    95	     * Returns an optional ServerConnectionId object from the given connection ID string.
    96	     *
    97	     * @param connectionIdString The connection ID string to parse.
    98	     * @return An optional ServerConnectionId object.
    99	     */
   100	    public static Optional<ServerConnectionId> fromString(String connectionIdString) {
   101	        String[] split = connectionIdString.split("-");
   102	        if (split.length != 4) {
   103	            return Optional.empty();
   104	        }
   105	
   106	        try {
   107	            return parseHostPort(split[0]).flatMap(localHost -> parseHostPort(split[1]).map(
   108	                remoteHost -> new ServerConnectionId(localHost, remoteHost, Integer.parseInt(split[2]), Integer.parseInt(split[3]))));
   109	        } catch (NumberFormatException e) {
   110	            return Optional.empty();
   111	        }
   112	    }
   113	
   114	    /**
   115	     * Generates a unique connection ID for the given socket.
   116	     *
   117	     * @param socket The socket for which the connection ID is to be generated.
   118	     * @param processorId The ID of the server processor that will handle this connection.
   119	     * @param connectionIndex The index to be used in the connection ID to ensure uniqueness.
   120	     * @return A string representing the unique connection ID.
   121	     */
   122	    public static String generateConnectionId(Socket socket, int processorId, int connectionIndex) {
   123	        String localHost = socket.getLocalAddress().getHostAddress();
   124	        int localPort = socket.getLocalPort();
   125	        String remoteHost = socket.getInetAddress().getHostAddress();
   126	        int remotePort = socket.getPort();
   127	        return localHost + ":" + localPort + "-" + remoteHost + ":" + remotePort + "-" + processorId + "-" + connectionIndex;
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    /**
   131	     * Map entry consists of host:port or ipv6_host:port
   132	     */
   133	    // Visible for testing
   134	    static Optional<Map.Entry<String, Integer>> parseHostPort(String connectionString) {
   135	        Matcher matcher = HOST_PORT_PARSE_EXP.matcher(connectionString);
   136	        if (matcher.matches()) {
   137	            try {
   138	                return Optional.of(Map.entry(matcher.group(1), Integer.parseInt(matcher.group(2))));
   139	            } catch (NumberFormatException e) {
   140	                // Ignore
   141	            }
   142	        }
   143	        return Optional.empty();
   144	    }
   145	}

     1	/*

    17	package org.apache.kafka.common.network;
    18	
    19	import org.apache.kafka.common.errors.AuthenticationException;
    20	
    21	/**
    22	 * States for KafkaChannel:
    23	 * <ul>
    24	 *   <li>NOT_CONNECTED: Connections are created in NOT_CONNECTED state. State is updated
    25	 *       on {@link TransportLayer#finishConnect()} when socket connection is established.
    26	 *       PLAINTEXT channels transition from NOT_CONNECTED to READY, others transition
    27	 *       to AUTHENTICATE. Failures in NOT_CONNECTED state typically indicate that the
    28	 *       remote endpoint is unavailable, which may be due to misconfigured endpoints.</li>
    29	 *   <li>AUTHENTICATE: SSL, SASL_SSL and SASL_PLAINTEXT channels are in AUTHENTICATE state during SSL and
    30	 *       SASL handshake. Disconnections in AUTHENTICATE state may indicate that authentication failed with
    31	 *       SSL or SASL (broker version < 1.0.0). Channels transition to READY state when authentication completes
    32	 *       successfully.</li>
    33	 *   <li>READY: Connected, authenticated channels are in READY state. Channels may transition from
    34	 *       READY to EXPIRED, FAILED_SEND or LOCAL_CLOSE.</li>
    35	 *   <li>EXPIRED: Idle connections are moved to EXPIRED state on idle timeout and the channel is closed.</li>
    36	 *   <li>FAILED_SEND: Channels transition from READY to FAILED_SEND state if the channel is closed due
    37	 *       to a send failure.</li>
    38	 *   <li>AUTHENTICATION_FAILED: Channels are moved to this state if the requested SASL mechanism is not
    39	 *       enabled in the broker or when brokers with versions 1.0.0 and above provide an error response
    40	 *       during SASL authentication. {@link #exception()} gives the reason provided by the broker for
    41	 *       authentication failure.</li>
    42	 *   <li>LOCAL_CLOSE: Channels are moved to LOCAL_CLOSE state if close() is initiated locally.</li>
    43	 * </ul>
    44	 * If the remote endpoint closes a channel, the state of the channel reflects the state the channel
    45	 * was in at the time of disconnection. This state may be useful to identify the reason for disconnection.
    46	 * <p>
    47	 * Typical transitions:
    48	 * <ul>
    49	 *   <li>PLAINTEXT Good path: NOT_CONNECTED => READY => LOCAL_CLOSE</li>
    50	 *   <li>SASL/SSL Good path: NOT_CONNECTED => AUTHENTICATE => READY => LOCAL_CLOSE</li>
    51	 *   <li>Bootstrap server misconfiguration: NOT_CONNECTED, disconnected in NOT_CONNECTED state</li>
    52	 *   <li>Security misconfiguration: NOT_CONNECTED => AUTHENTICATE => AUTHENTICATION_FAILED, disconnected in AUTHENTICATION_FAILED state</li>
    53	 *   <li>Security misconfiguration with older broker: NOT_CONNECTED => AUTHENTICATE, disconnected in AUTHENTICATE state</li>
    54	 * </ul>
    55	 */
    56	public class ChannelState {
    57	    public enum State {
    58	        NOT_CONNECTED,
    59	        AUTHENTICATE,
    60	        READY,
    61	        EXPIRED,
    62	        FAILED_SEND,
    63	        AUTHENTICATION_FAILED,
    64	        LOCAL_CLOSE
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    // AUTHENTICATION_FAILED has a custom exception. For other states,
    68	    // create a reusable `ChannelState` instance per-state.
    69	    public static final ChannelState NOT_CONNECTED = new ChannelState(State.NOT_CONNECTED);
    70	    public static final ChannelState AUTHENTICATE = new ChannelState(State.AUTHENTICATE);
    71	    public static final ChannelState READY = new ChannelState(State.READY);
    72	    public static final ChannelState EXPIRED = new ChannelState(State.EXPIRED);
    73	    public static final ChannelState FAILED_SEND = new ChannelState(State.FAILED_SEND);
    74	    public static final ChannelState LOCAL_CLOSE = new ChannelState(State.LOCAL_CLOSE);
    75	
    76	    private final State state;
    77	    private final AuthenticationException exception;
    78	    private final String remoteAddress;
    79	
    80	    public ChannelState(State state) {
    81	        this(state, null, null);
    82	    }
    83	
    84	    public ChannelState(State state, String remoteAddress) {
    85	        this(state, null, remoteAddress);
    86	    }
    87	    
    88	    public ChannelState(State state, AuthenticationException exception, String remoteAddress) {
    89	        this.state = state;
    90	        this.exception = exception;
    91	        this.remoteAddress = remoteAddress;
    92	    }
    93	
    94	    public State state() {
    95	        return state;
    96	    }
    97	
    98	    public AuthenticationException exception() {
    99	        return exception;
   100	    }
   101	
   102	    public String remoteAddress() {
   103	        return remoteAddress;
   104	    }
   105	}

   865	  @Test
   866	  def testConnectionRatePerIp(): Unit = {
   867	    val defaultTimeoutMs = 2000
   868	    val overrideProps = TestUtils.createBrokerConfig(0, port = 0)
   869	    overrideProps.remove(SocketServerConfigs.MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_IP_CONFIG)
   870	    overrideProps.put(QuotaConfig.NUM_QUOTA_SAMPLES_CONFIG, String.valueOf(2))
   871	    val connectionRate = 5
   872	    val time = new MockTime()
   873	    val overrideServer = new SocketServer(KafkaConfig.fromProps(overrideProps), new Metrics(),
   874	      time, credentialProvider, apiVersionManager)
   875	    // update the connection rate to 5
   876	    overrideServer.connectionQuotas.updateIpConnectionRateQuota(None, Some(connectionRate))
   877	    try {
   878	      overrideServer.enableRequestProcessing(Map.empty).get(1, TimeUnit.MINUTES)
   879	      // make the (maximum allowable number + 1) of connections
   880	      (0 to connectionRate).map(_ => connect(overrideServer))
   881	
   882	      val acceptors = overrideServer.dataPlaneAcceptors.asScala.values
   883	      // waiting for 5 connections got accepted and 1 connection got throttled
   884	      TestUtils.waitUntilTrue(
   885	        () => acceptors.foldLeft(0)((accumulator, acceptor) => accumulator + acceptor.throttledSockets.size) == 1,
   886	        "timeout waiting for 1 connection to get throttled",
   887	        defaultTimeoutMs)
   888	
   889	      // now try one more, so that we can make sure this connection will get throttled
   890	      var conn = connect(overrideServer)
   891	      // there should be total 2 connection got throttled now
   892	      TestUtils.waitUntilTrue(
   893	        () => acceptors.foldLeft(0)((accumulator, acceptor) => accumulator + acceptor.throttledSockets.size) == 2,
   894	        "timeout waiting for 2 connection to get throttled",
   895	        defaultTimeoutMs)
   896	      // advance time to unthrottle connections
   897	      time.sleep(defaultTimeoutMs)
   898	      acceptors.foreach(_.wakeup())
   899	      // make sure there are no connection got throttled now(and the throttled connections should be closed)
   900	      TestUtils.waitUntilTrue(() => acceptors.forall(_.throttledSockets.isEmpty),
   901	        "timeout waiting for connection to be unthrottled",
   902	        defaultTimeoutMs)
   903	      // verify the connection is closed now
   904	      verifyRemoteConnectionClosed(conn)
   905	
   906	      // new connection should succeed after previous connection closed, and previous samples have been expired
   907	      conn = connect(overrideServer)
   908	      val serializedBytes = producerRequestBytes()
   909	      sendRequest(conn, serializedBytes)
   910	      val request = overrideServer.dataPlaneRequestChannel.receiveRequest(defaultTimeoutMs)

codex
I found a startup/lifecycle issue that is stronger than a tuning concern: the io_uring listener binds during Processor construction, while the NIO path intentionally defers opening the socket until `enableRequestProcessing` releases the acceptor after authorizer startup. I’m doing one last pass for metric/state divergences so the verdict separates blockers from non-blocking parity gaps.

    17	package org.apache.kafka.network.iouring;
    18	
    19	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.ChannelMetadataRegistry;
    20	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.CipherInformation;
    21	import org.apache.kafka.common.network.ClientInformation;
    22	
    23	/**
    24	 * Per-channel metadata holder used by {@link IoUringSelector}.
    25	 *
    26	 * <p>The NIO {@code Selector} ties its inner-class registry to its own metrics sensors so
    27	 * {@code connectionsByCipher} / {@code connectionsByClient} sensors track active counts.
    28	 * v1 of the io_uring backend deliberately does not wire those broker-wide gauges — the
    29	 * sensors live on {@code Selector} and re-creating them would mean leaking
    30	 * implementation-private state out of {@code clients/}. We hold the last-registered
    31	 * values so {@code KafkaChannel.channelMetadataRegistry().clientInformation()} returns
    32	 * sensible data to the request-handling path; the matching metrics will land alongside
    33	 * the broker's broader telemetry wiring (separate follow-up).
    34	 */
    35	final class IoUringChannelMetadataRegistry implements ChannelMetadataRegistry {
    36	
    37	    private CipherInformation cipherInformation;
    38	    private ClientInformation clientInformation;
    39	
    40	    @Override
    41	    public void registerCipherInformation(CipherInformation cipherInformation) {
    42	        this.cipherInformation = cipherInformation;
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    @Override
    46	    public CipherInformation cipherInformation() {
    47	        return cipherInformation;
    48	    }
    49	
    50	    @Override
    51	    public void registerClientInformation(ClientInformation clientInformation) {
    52	        this.clientInformation = clientInformation;
    53	    }
    54	
    55	    @Override
    56	    public ClientInformation clientInformation() {
    57	        return clientInformation;
    58	    }
    59	
    60	    @Override
    61	    public void close() {
    62	        cipherInformation = null;
    63	        clientInformation = null;
    64	    }
    65	}

?? .claude/

  ✓ Read spec and recent patch trajectory
  ✓ Map io_uring production code and SocketServer wiring
  ✓ Compare invariants against NIO Selector and Processor paths
  ✓ Check blocker classes: quotas, slowloris, mute, idle expiry, lifecycle, races
  → Deliver verdict with file:line citations
codex
**Findings**

1. **BLOCKER: io_uring binds before Kafka’s request-processing/authorizer gate.** `SocketServer` creates/configures acceptors before `enableRequestProcessing` completes, and `configure()` creates Processors immediately: [SocketServer.scala:218](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:218>), [SocketServer.scala:472](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:472>). The Processor constructor path builds `IoUringServerListener`, whose constructor binds synchronously: [SocketServer.scala:934](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:934>), [SocketServer.scala:971](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:971>), [IoUringServerListener.java:151](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/server/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/network/iouring/IoUringServerListener.java:151>). NIO explicitly defers opening non-wildcard sockets until `Acceptor.start()` because open sockets signal readiness: [SocketServer.scala:508](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:508>), and `enableRequestProcessing` only calls `acceptor.start()` after the authorizer future completes: [SocketServer.scala:190](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:190>). This means a Linux PLAINTEXT `auto` listener can accept TCP connections before request processing is enabled or after authorizer startup fails.

2. **BLOCKER: mute/unmute can bypass the inbound direct-memory watermark.** The transport disables Netty `autoRead` when per-channel queued inbound bytes reach the high watermark: [IoUringTransportLayer.java:91](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/server/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/network/iouring/IoUringTransportLayer.java:91>), [IoUringTransportLayer.java:218](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/server/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/network/iouring/IoUringTransportLayer.java:218>). The read path only re-enables `autoRead` after the queue drops below the low watermark: [IoUringTransportLayer.java:349](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/server/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/network/iouring/IoUringTransportLayer.java:349>). But `addInterestOps(OP_READ)` re-enables `autoRead` unconditionally on unmute, without checking `inboundBytes`: [IoUringTransportLayer.java:288](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/server/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/network/iouring/IoUringTransportLayer.java:288>). That path is used by `KafkaChannel.maybeUnmute()` and `IoUringSelector.unmute()`: [KafkaChannel.java:265](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/network/KafkaChannel.java:265>), [IoUringSelector.java:821](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/server/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/network/iouring/IoUringSelector.java:821>). A pipelined or malicious client can keep the inbound queue above the high watermark and have every response-cycle unmute reopen Netty reads before the Processor drains below the low watermark.

3. **CORRECTNESS: key NIO selector metrics are missing or misleading.** The io_uring selector is constructed without `Metrics` or `metricTags`: [SocketServer.scala:949](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:949>). The NIO selector is constructed with them: [SocketServer.scala:1045](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:1045>). `NetworkProcessorAvgIdlePercent` reads `io-wait-ratio` and returns `0.0` when absent: [SocketServer.scala:911](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/core/src/main/scala/kafka/network/SocketServer.scala:911>). NIO creates and records the selector sensors, including `io-wait`, `io`, byte, request, and connection metrics: [Selector.java:1151](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/network/Selector.java:1151>), [Selector.java:1230](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/network/Selector.java:1230>). The io_uring metadata registry also documents that client/cipher connection gauges are not wired: [IoUringChannelMetadataRegistry.java:26](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/server/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/network/iouring/IoUringChannelMetadataRegistry.java:26>).

4. **CORRECTNESS: remote disconnect state diverges from NIO.** io_uring maps closing-channel eviction to `ChannelState.LOCAL_CLOSE`: [IoUringSelector.java:648](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/server/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/network/iouring/IoUringSelector.java:648>). NIO’s graceful remote-close path reports the channel’s existing state via `disconnected.put(channel.id(), channel.state())`: [Selector.java:929](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/network/Selector.java:929>), [Selector.java:970](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/network/Selector.java:970>). `ChannelState` documents that remote closes should reflect the state at disconnection time: [ChannelState.java:44](</home/florent/kafka-current-london-2026/.claude/worktrees/io-uring/clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/network/ChannelState.java:44>).

I did not modify code. I did not run tests; this was a read-only production-readiness audit.
